Data sources and transparency

Last reviewed: 2026-10-30. This page lists every operator source this project uses or plans to use, how each one publishes its data, the rate limits that apply, and what has been found while reading the data. It reports what was observed and when. It does not say whether anyone has met their legal duties.

Sources

OCPI 2.2.1 says a location whose publish flag is false may not be shown on a website or app, so those are never shown. Locations with no flag are not shown either, unless the repository owner has recorded a decision for that operator, with the reason and date below. A flag set to false is always respected.

Sources and how each one publishes its data

Aldi Stores LimitedKey issued on requestYes. Prices that give no VAT rate have VAT at 20% added, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-29: Aldi Stores Limited's 3 tariffs on Eco-Movement's PCPR API gave no VAT rate on 2026-10-29. Its energy prices are 0.333, 0.492 and 0.517 pounds per kWh, which are 40p, 59p and 62p with 20% added, the UK standard rate of VAT on public charging. Drax's announcement of 10 June 2026 that it operates Aldi's charge point network says "Charging will be competitively priced at 40p per kWh for fast charging and 62p per kWh for rapid charging", which matches the feed prices with 20% added. That the prices exclude VAT is inferred from the figures, not stated in the feed. The repository owner decided on 2026-10-29 that VAT at 20% is added, so prices on the map all include VAT and can be compared. (evidence)locations: https://open-chargepoints.com/api/ocpi/cpo/2.2.1/locations/
tariffs: https://open-chargepoints.com/api/ocpi/cpo/2.2.1/tariffs/
statuses: https://open-chargepoints.com/api/statuses		OGL-3.0, terms checked 2026-10-29Not shown2026-10-27: Three emails from Eco-Movement on 2026-10-27, each with expiring links to tokens for the same eight networks, this one among them, for its PCPR API, sent as an "Authorization: Token" header to its OCPI 2.2.1 endpoints. The token itself is not kept here.
AllegoKey issued on requestYes. Prices that give no VAT rate have VAT at 20% added, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-29: Allego's three tariffs on Eco-Movement's PCPR API gave no VAT rate on 2026-10-29. One gives an energy price of 0.525 pounds per kWh with the text "Energy: 0.63 GBP/kWh", and another 0.7 pounds with "Energy: 0.84 GBP/kWh": each text price is the feed price with 20% added, the UK standard rate of VAT on public charging. All three energy prices come to whole pence once 20% is added. That the prices exclude VAT is inferred from the figures, not stated by Allego. The repository owner decided on 2026-10-29 that VAT at 20% is added, so prices on the map all include VAT and can be compared. (evidence). No floor level is shown for its charge points, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-30: Allego's 240 EVSEs on Eco-Movement's PCPR API on 2026-10-30 give a floor_level of "Alle" (158), "GB, " (80) or "UK, " (2): the first four characters of location names such as "Allego - Hollywood Bowl Shrewsbury" and "GB, Station Street, E15 1DA London", not the level the charge point is on. The repository owner decided on 2026-10-30 that no floor level is shown for Allego's charge points. (evidence)locations: https://open-chargepoints.com/api/ocpi/cpo/2.2.1/locations/

Blink ChargingKey issued on requestYes. AC connectors that give 400 V and no power figure have their power worked out at 230 V, each saying so, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-30: Blink Charging gives every connector's maximum power as 0, so its power is worked out from voltage and current. In its feed of 2026-10-29, 384 of its 3,493 AC connectors gave 400 V, the voltage between two lines of a UK three-phase supply, and 288 of them said they were single phase. OCPI 2.2.1 gives AC voltage line to neutral, 230 V in the UK, so a 32 A single-phase socket worked out at 12.8 kW rather than 7.4 kW, and a 32 A three-phase one at 38.4 kW rather than 22 kW. That 230 V is meant is inferred, not stated by Blink Charging. The repository owner decided on 2026-10-30 that these connectors are worked out at 230 V, each saying so. (evidence). Prices that give no VAT rate have VAT at 20% added, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-29: Blink Charging's tariffs on Eco-Movement's PCPR API gave no VAT rate on any price on 2026-10-29. Of the 57 energy prices in its 56 tariffs, 56 come to whole pence once 20% is added (for example 0.375 pounds per kWh becomes 45p), while 12 are whole pence as published. This suggests prices with VAT at 20%, the UK standard rate on public charging, taken off; that is inferred from the figures, not stated by Blink Charging. The repository owner decided on 2026-10-29 that VAT at 20% is added, so prices on the map all include VAT and can be compared. (evidence)locations: https://open-chargepoints.com/api/ocpi/cpo/2.2.1/locations/
tariffs: https://open-chargepoints.com/api/ocpi/cpo/2.2.1/tariffs/
statuses: https://open-chargepoints.com/api/statuses		OGL-3.0, terms checked 2026-10-29Not shown2026-10-10: Blink Charging's open data request page links a request form and says requests are shared with Eco-Movement and that the data is provided in API format. It states no licence, terms or rate limit for the data.
2026-10-10: The linked form, titled "PCPR Data Request - Blink Charging", asks for an email address, name, organisation and planned use of the data, and says an answer is given within 5 working days.
2026-10-26: Eco-Movement's email with an expiring link to this network's token for its PCPR API, sent as an "Authorization: Token" header to its OCPI 2.2.1 endpoints. The token itself is not kept here.

Its energy prices are 0.542 and 0.742 pounds per kWh, which are 65p and 89p with 20% added, the UK standard rate of VAT on public charging, to the nearest penny. bp pulse's own site page for a charger in Slough (read 2026-10-30) lists pay as you go prices of "£0.65" for "Fast (AC 7kW)" and "£0.79 - £0.93" for "Rapid (AC 43kW / DC 50kW)", without saying whether VAT is included. That the feed's prices exclude VAT is inferred from the figures, not stated by bp pulse. BrightGreenOpen, no registrationYes. Coordinates that are obviously the wrong way round, or obviously missing a minus sign, are corrected and marked on the map, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-23: Where a location's longitude is obviously missing its minus sign, or its latitude and longitude are obviously the wrong way round, the repository owner decided to show it in the right place rather than leave it off the map, with the operator's own figures kept and the correction shown on the map and recorded in the findings below. Kiln Farm Station's point as published is inside the UK, so the owner named it here on the same day (see findings). Named by the owner as missing a minus sign although the point as published is inside the UK: Kiln Farm Station (MK11 3LW, id 4cbfa56a-07da-4cf1-90be-8277667b4158). The VAT each price states is read as already in the price, so it is not added again, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-23: BrightGreen's tariffs file gives every price with VAT 20 and also a display price, "price_display", equal to the price as written: for example price "0.59", VAT 20 and price_display "£0.59" (12 of 12 tariffs on 23 October 2026). Read as OCPI 2.2.1 says, 0.59 is 70.8p per kWh including VAT, while the display price shows 59p; which one drivers pay is not stated. The repository owner decided that the prices are read as the display price shows them, already including VAT, so no VAT is added. (evidence). Where a connector gives no plug type (standard), its socket_type is read: “Type 2” as IEC_62196_T2, “CCS 2” as IEC_62196_T2_COMBO, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-30: BrightGreen's connectors give no OCPI 2.2.1 standard, so their plug type was unknown. Each gives a socket_type, a field outside OCPI 2.2.1: "Type 2" on 50 and "CCS 2" on 2 of the 52 connectors at published locations on 2026-10-30. These are the common names of the plugs OCPI 2.2.1 calls IEC_62196_T2 (IEC 62196 Type 2 "Mennekes") and IEC_62196_T2_COMBO (Combo Type 2 based, DC). The repository owner decided on 2026-10-30 that socket_type is read as the plug type for these two values. (evidence). Where a charge point's status is not an OCPI 2.2.1 value, it is taken from its connectors' status when they all give the same OCPI 2.2.1 status, and is unknown otherwise, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-30: Every EVSE status is "idle", which is not an OCPI 2.2.1 value, so every charge point showed status unknown. Each connector also gives a status, a field outside OCPI 2.2.1, mostly in OCPI 2.2.1 words with a capital first letter: "Available" (44), "Charging" (3), "Unknown" (4) and "Unavailable" (1) on 2026-10-30. OCPI 2.2.1 describes its statuses as those of the "EVSE/Connector". The repository owner decided on 2026-10-30 that a charge point's status is taken from its connectors where they all give the same OCPI 2.2.1 status (28 of 30 charge points at published locations on 2026-10-30), and is unknown otherwise. (evidence)locations: https://connect.bright.green/ocpp/locations
tariffs: https://connect.bright.green/ocpp/tariffs		OGL-3.0, terms checked 2026-10-23Not shown2026-10-23: Links "Locations Data" and "Tariffs Data" on connect.bright.green and states no licence, terms or rate limit.
char.gyOpen, no registrationYes. Statuses that are not OCPI values are shown by the owner's decision of 2026-10-09: char.gy publishes EVSE statuses as WORKING and FAULTED, which are not OCPI 2.2.1 values. Regulation 10(6)(a) uses "working" to mean an OCPI status of available, charging or reserved, so WORKING is shown as working, free or in use not stated, and never as available. FAULTED is shown as reported out of service. The repository owner chose this reading. (evidence). Prices that give no VAT rate have VAT at 20% added, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-22: char.gy's feed gives no VAT rate on any price. Its own pricing page (https://char.gy/pricing, checked 9 October 2026) gives 39p, 59p and 54p per kWh where the feed gives 0.325, 0.4917 and 0.45 pounds: each feed price is the page price less 20%, the UK standard rate of VAT on public charging. The repository owner decided that VAT at 20% is added, so prices on the map all include VAT and can be compared. (evidence)locations: https://char.gy/open-ocpi/locations
tariffs: https://char.gy/open-ocpi/tariffs		OGL-3.0, terms checked 2026-10-07Not shown2026-10-06
2026-10-07: Re-checked. The page still lists the open locations and tariffs endpoints.
ChargePlace ScotlandOpen, no registrationYes. Connectors that name a tariff only Evolt Network publishes are priced from that exact tariff, never by name, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-10: Some ChargePlace Scotland connectors name tariffs that only Evolt Network's feed holds. The repository owner decided that a connector may be priced from Evolt Network's tariff only when it is definitely the same tariff for that site and the two operators are related, never because of a coincidental tariff name: the connector names that exact tariff id, which is a UUID, ChargePlace Scotland's own response does not have it, and Evolt Network's response has it once, with the same country code. ChargePlace Scotland's Scheme Terms say SWARCO Smart Charging Ltd operates the network, Evolt Network's terms say SWARCO Smart Charging owns and operates its network, and both feeds are published on info.smartcharging.uk. (evidence)locations: https://info.smartcharging.uk/public_feed/locations/2463

The answer mentions no registration step, key, licence or rate limit. Both feeds answered without a key on 2026-10-10.

While its own locations are on the map, the locations Monta sends under its country and party codes are left off and listed on the feed health page, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-23: Monta's open data feed also carries Urban Fox locations under Urban Fox's own party, GB UFX, with different location ids: 95 of them were on the map on 23 October 2026, and 39 of the first 50 locations in Urban Fox's own feed are within about 30 metres of one of them. The repository owner decided that Urban Fox's own feed is shown in their place, and that Monta's GB UFX locations are left off while Urban Fox's own locations, from the latest run or its last good copy, are on the map. Access and engagement

How this project has tried to reach each operator’s data, with the date of every step. Each status is dated by the latest check or step recorded for that operator, and requests and responses are shown by the dates they were sent and received. “None recorded” means no response has been recorded here; it says nothing about why. No personal names or contact details are published. Any operator can correct or reply to an entry by opening an issue, and replies are added to its log.

Rate limits

The Public Charge Point Regulations 2023 set no limit on how often data users may request the data. Regulation 10(5) requires the data to be available free of charge without terms on its use. DfT's guidance allows terms covering the means of access, which is where operators' rate limits come from.

  • “terms and conditions covering the means of access to the data and API(s) are permissible” (DfT, Public Charge Point Regulations 2023 guidance)

This project never sends more than one request per second to any operator's host, keeps within every limit an operator, or the company hosting its data, publishes with a safety margin on top, and waits as long as a server asks before contacting it again. Gaps are measured from the end of the previous request and shared by every operator on the same host, unless the repository owner has decided that a host's published limits apply to each key: then each operator keeps its gap for its own key, and the host still gets one request at a time. Automated tests check these rules.

Rate limits: what each operator publishes and what this project does
OperatorPublished limit (operator or its data host)This project’s settingComparisonCompared with the regulations
3ti1 per 30 seconds for all requests, published by 3ti: “Fuuse allows roughly 1-2 requests per 30 seconds per IP (shared across sites and endpoints), so this script makes ONE request at a time, at least 31 s apart” (source, checked 2026-10-23)At least 31 s between requests to the operator's host, measured from the end of the previous request (at most 117 in any hour), retries included.Within every published limit. The strictest needs 30 s between requests; this project waits 31 s, which includes a safety margin.
The limit is quoted from a comment in the script on 3ti's public charge points page, read as its lower figure, 1 request per 30 seconds. Arnold Clark Charge and ScottishPower Recharge use the same 31 second gap on the same host, and the client keeps one timer for it. Five sites are read, each with a location and a tariff file, so a run makes 10 requests and takes about 5 minutes.		The operator sets a limit; the regulations set none. DfT guidance allows such terms on access.
Aldi Stores Limited30 per 1 hour for the locations endpoint, published by Eco-Movement: “The `locations` endpoint has a rate limit of 30 requests per 1 hour.” (source, checked 2026-10-26)
30 per 1 hour for the locations endpoint (single location), published by Eco-Movement: “The `locations/{id}` endpoint has a rate limit of 30 requests per 1 hour.” (source, checked 2026-10-26)
30 per 1 hour for the tariffs endpoint, published by Eco-Movement: “The `tariffs` endpoint has a rate limit of 30 requests per 1 hour.” (source, checked 2026-10-26)
1 per 30 seconds for the statuses endpoint, published by Eco-Movement: “The `statuses` endpoint has a rate limit of 1 request per 30 seconds.” (source, checked 2026-10-26)		Each published limit is kept as an allowance for the operator's own key, by the owner's decisions of 2026-10-29 (limits per key) and 2026-10-29 (an allowance): no period of a limit's length plus 1 s holds more than one request fewer than it allows, retries included, and each request with the key starts at least 2 s after its last one finished. At most 5 requests go to the host at the same time, started at least 1 s apart. Any retry, wait the server asks for or refusal brings back 125 s between requests with each key (at most 29 in any hour with it), one request at a time at the host, for the rest of the run.Within every published limit, keeping one request in hand in each limit's period as a safety margin; after any sign of trouble, 125 s between requests, which includes a safety margin.
By the repository owner's decisions of 2026-10-29 the limits are read as applying to each network's own token (limits_per_key) and as an allowance rather than a spacing (limits_as_allowance). With this network's token, no period of an hour and a second holds more than 29 requests to an endpoint limited to 30 an hour, and each request starts at least 2 seconds after the last one finished. At most 5 requests are sent to open-chargepoints.com at the same time, whichever network they are for, started at least 1 second apart. Any retry, wait the server asks for or refusal puts every network back to a gap of 125 seconds (the 120 seconds the strictest limit needs, plus a safety margin) and one request at a time for the rest of the run. Eco-Movement "strongly recommends limiting simultaneous GET requests to a maximum of 5". It recommends retrieving the entire dataset once a day.
Limits read per key by the owner's decision of 2026-10-29: Eco-Movement publishes a limit of 30 requests an hour for each of the locations and tariffs endpoints and gives each network its own token, but does not say whether the limits count per token or per address. The repository owner decided on 2026-10-29 that they are read as applying to each token, so each network keeps its own 125 second gap, while requests to open-chargepoints.com are still sent one at a time. (evidence)
Limits read as an allowance, at most 5 requests at once, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-29: Eco-Movement publishes its limits as a number of requests an hour (30 for each of the locations and tariffs endpoints) and does not say they must be spread evenly. The repository owner decided on 2026-10-29 that each network's token may use that allowance close together once a day, as the whole dataset fits within it (the largest network, ubitricity, took 14 requests on 2026-10-29), keeping one request in hand as the margin. Eco-Movement's PCPR API user guide "strongly recommends limiting simultaneous GET requests to a maximum of 5". It does not say whether that counts per token or in total; the owner chose 5 in total, which keeps within either reading. (evidence)		The operator sets a limit; the regulations set none. DfT guidance allows such terms on access.

Arnold Clark's pages state no rate limit. The gap is this project's own choice: 2 seconds until 11 October 2026, then 31 seconds, because the host answered the second request of each run with HTTP 429 and Retry-After 30 (see findings). 31 seconds is the 30 the host asks for, plus a 1 second margin. ScottishPower Recharge's files are on the same host and use the same gap. Each response holds every record, so a full run makes two requests (about 0.3 MB on 2026-10-10).		Neither the operator nor the regulations set a limit.

Limits read per key by the owner's decision of 2026-10-29: Eco-Movement publishes a limit of 30 requests an hour for each of the locations and tariffs endpoints and gives each network its own token, but does not say whether the limits count per token or per address. The repository owner decided on 2026-10-29 that they are read as applying to each token, so each network keeps its own 125 second gap, while requests to open-chargepoints.com are still sent one at a time. (evidence)
Limits read as an allowance, at most 5 requests at once, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-29: Eco-Movement publishes its limits as a number of requests an hour (30 for each of the locations and tariffs endpoints) and does not say they must be spread evenly. The repository owner decided on 2026-10-29 that each network's token may use that allowance close together once a day, as the whole dataset fits within it (the largest network, ubitricity, took 14 requests on 2026-10-29), keeping one request in hand as the margin. Eco-Movement's PCPR API user guide "strongly recommends limiting simultaneous GET requests to a maximum of 5". It does not say whether that counts per token or in total; the owner chose 5 in total, which keeps within either reading. (evidence)		The operator sets a limit; the regulations set none. DfT guidance allows such terms on access.

The gap is this project's own choice: 2 seconds until 8 October 2026, then 6 seconds after the feed refused requests from GitHub's servers part-way through the first daily run (see findings). Lowered to 4 seconds on 9 October 2026 by the repository owner's decision, to shorten the daily run, on the condition that it goes back to 6 seconds if the feed refused requests again. 30 per 1 hour for the tariffs endpoint, published by Eco-Movement: “The `tariffs` endpoint has a rate limit of 30 requests per 1 hour.” (source, checked 2026-10-26)
1 per 30 seconds for the statuses endpoint, published by Eco-Movement: “The `statuses` endpoint has a rate limit of 1 request per 30 seconds.” (source, checked 2026-10-26)		Each published limit is kept as an allowance for the operator's own key, by the owner's decisions of 2026-10-29 (limits per key) and 2026-10-29 (an allowance): no period of a limit's length plus 1 s holds more than one request fewer than it allows, retries included, and each request with the key starts at least 2 s after its last one finished. At most 5 requests go to the host at the same time, started at least 1 s apart. Any retry, wait the server asks for or refusal brings back 125 s between requests with each key (at most 29 in any hour with it), one request at a time at the host, for the rest of the run.Within every published limit, keeping one request in hand in each limit's period as a safety margin; after any sign of trouble, 125 s between requests, which includes a safety margin.
By the repository owner's decisions of 2026-10-29 the limits are read as applying to each network's own token (limits_per_key) and as an allowance rather than a spacing (limits_as_allowance). With this network's token, no period of an hour and a second holds more than 29 requests to an endpoint limited to 30 an hour, and each request starts at least 2 seconds after the last one finished. At most 5 requests are sent to open-chargepoints.com at the same time, whichever network they are for, started at least 1 second apart. Any retry, wait the server asks for or refusal puts every network back to a gap of 125 seconds (the 120 seconds the strictest limit needs, plus a safety margin) and one request at a time for the rest of the run. Eco-Movement "strongly recommends limiting simultaneous GET requests to a maximum of 5". It recommends retrieving the entire dataset once a day.
Limits read per key by the owner's decision of 2026-10-29: Eco-Movement publishes a limit of 30 requests an hour for each of the locations and tariffs endpoints and gives each network its own token, but does not say whether the limits count per token or per address. The repository owner decided on 2026-10-29 that they are read as applying to each token, so each network keeps its own 125 second gap, while requests to open-chargepoints.com are still sent one at a time. (evidence)
Limits read as an allowance, at most 5 requests at once, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-29: Eco-Movement publishes its limits as a number of requests an hour (30 for each of the locations and tariffs endpoints) and does not say they must be spread evenly. The repository owner decided on 2026-10-29 that each network's token may use that allowance close together once a day, as the whole dataset fits within it (the largest network, ubitricity, took 14 requests on 2026-10-29), keeping one request in hand as the margin. Eco-Movement's PCPR API user guide "strongly recommends limiting simultaneous GET requests to a maximum of 5". It does not say whether that counts per token or in total; the owner chose 5 in total, which keeps within either reading. (evidence)		The operator sets a limit; the regulations set none. DfT guidance allows such terms on access.
FastnedNone published (checked 2026-10-24)At least 2 s between requests to the operator's host, measured from the end of the previous request (at most 1800 in any hour), retries included.No limit published (checked 2026-10-24). The gap is our own choice.
Fastned's Fair Use Policy, read 2026-10-10 and again 2026-10-24, states no numeric limit. It says "Fastned may throttle your use of the APIs at any time." and "Fastned reserves the right to implement rate limits on the API to ensure system performance, reliability, and overall service quality or for other reasons at its discretion." The key email gives no limit either, and the responses of 2026-10-24 carried no rate limit headers. The 2 second gap is this project's own choice, and the feed is fetched once a day: two requests a run.		Neither the operator nor the regulations set a limit.

GeniePoint's open data page states no rate limit. The 2 second gap is this project's own choice. Each file holds every record, so a full run makes two requests (about 1.6 MB on 2026-10-08).		Neither the operator nor the regulations set a limit.

Eco-Movement "strongly recommends limiting simultaneous GET requests to a maximum of 5". It recommends retrieving the entire dataset once a day.
Limits read per key by the owner's decision of 2026-10-29: Eco-Movement publishes a limit of 30 requests an hour for each of the locations and tariffs endpoints and gives each network its own token, but does not say whether the limits count per token or per address. The repository owner decided on 2026-10-29 that they are read as applying to each token, so each network keeps its own 125 second gap, while requests to open-chargepoints.com are still sent one at a time. (evidence)
Limits read as an allowance, at most 5 requests at once, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-29: Eco-Movement publishes its limits as a number of requests an hour (30 for each of the locations and tariffs endpoints) and does not say they must be spread evenly. The repository owner decided on 2026-10-29 that each network's token may use that allowance close together once a day, as the whole dataset fits within it (the largest network, ubitricity, took 14 requests on 2026-10-29), keeping one request in hand as the margin. Eco-Movement's PCPR API user guide "strongly recommends limiting simultaneous GET requests to a maximum of 5". It does not say whether that counts per token or in total; the owner chose 5 in total, which keeps within either reading. (evidence)		The operator sets a limit; the regulations set none. DfT guidance allows such terms on access.

At most 5 requests go to the host at the same time, started at least 1 s apart. Any retry, wait the server asks for or refusal brings back 125 s between requests with each key (at most 29 in any hour with it), one request at a time at the host, for the rest of the run.Within every published limit, keeping one request in hand in each limit's period as a safety margin; after any sign of trouble, 125 s between requests, which includes a safety margin.

The strictest needs 30 s between requests; this project waits 31 s, which includes a safety margin.
Gridserve's API Fair Use Policy sets the limit above, says "Default rate limits are set to 1 call every 30 seconds", and says limits may change. This project waits 31 seconds between any two requests to the host (30 seconds plus a safety margin). The developer documentation (version 1.0, November 2024) states no limit.		The operator sets a limit; the regulations set none. DfT guidance allows such terms on access.

What has been found

Differences from the OCPI 2.2.1 standard, quirks in the data and access issues, each with the date it was seen and what this project does about it. Values that are not valid are shown as unknown, never guessed. Only prices in pounds sterling are shown.

3ti
DateTypeWhat was foundHow this project handles itStatusEvidence
2026-10-23Documentation3ti publishes its data per site rather than as one feed: each of six sites listed on its page has its own location and tariff address on Fuuse's open data host.The pairs of addresses are read in turn and their records added together; each record keeps the address it came from.Openevidence
2026-10-23AccessOn 2026-10-23 the location and tariff addresses for Segen, Medway answered HTTP 204 with no content (the location address twice, about 8 minutes apart). The other five sites answered with OCPI responses.Segen's two addresses are not read, so its site is not on the map. They are listed in sources and will be checked again.Openevidence
2026-10-23Differs from OCPI 2.2.1Locations and tariffs carry no country_code or party_id, which OCPI 2.2.1 requires. EV Roam's register lists 3ti Energy Hubs as GB*3TI.Record ids use "unknown" for the country and party, from this file, until the operator's own data gives them.Openevidence
2026-10-23Differs from OCPI 2.2.1Tariff prices and VAT rates are sent as strings (for example "0.2917" and "20.0000") rather than numbers.Read as the numbers they spell; the stated 20% VAT is added, so the map's prices match the VAT-inclusive prices on 3ti's page.Openevidence
Aldi Stores Limited
DateTypeWhat was foundHow this project handles itStatusEvidence
2026-10-29Data quirkNo price gives a VAT rate. The energy prices are 0.333, 0.492 and 0.517 pounds per kWh, which are 40p, 59p and 62p with 20% added.By the repository owner's decision of 2026-10-29 recorded in vat_not_stated, VAT at 20% is added to every price that gives no VAT rate, and the price says this project added it. Reading the prices as excluding VAT is inferred from the figures and needs confirming by the network's operator. The tariffs are published unchanged.Openevidence
Allego
DateTypeWhat was foundHow this project handles itStatusEvidence
2026-10-29Data quirkNo price gives a VAT rate. The tariff text gives each energy price with 20% added (0.525 pounds per kWh and "Energy: 0.63 GBP/kWh").By the repository owner's decision of 2026-10-29 recorded in vat_not_stated, VAT at 20% is added to every price that gives no VAT rate, and the price says this project added it. Reading the prices as excluding VAT is inferred from the figures and needs confirming by Allego. The tariffs are published unchanged.Openevidence
2026-10-29Differs from OCPI 2.2.1240 EVSEs give parking_restrictions as a single string, such as "ev_only,plugged", where OCPI 2.2.1 has a list of values.Until 2026-10-29 the value was not read. From 2026-10-30 it is read as the list of the values it names, in upper case as OCPI 2.2.1 writes them (EV_ONLY and PLUGGED), and shown with each charge point; a word that is not an OCPI 2.2.1 value is still ignored. Nothing else in the record changes.Openevidence
2026-10-29Data quirkEvery connector gives max_electric_power 0, which OCPI 2.2.1 defines as the connector's maximum power in watts.A power of 0 is not shown. As for any connector with no power figure, its power is worked out from max_voltage and max_amperage, which Eco-Movement's documentation says it validates and corrects.Openevidence
2026-10-30Data quirkAll 240 EVSEs give a floor_level that is the first four characters of the location's name, such as "Alle" and "GB, ", where OCPI 2.2.1 has the level the charge point is on.By the repository owner's decision of 2026-10-30 recorded in floor_level_not_read, no floor level is shown for Allego's charge points. Nothing else in the record changes.Openevidence

2026-10-30Data quirkThe feed holds two kinds of location record. 696 of the 1,136 locations have numeric ids, EVSE ids beginning GB*BPC*, time_zone "UTC" and no opening times, and every connector gives max_electric_power 0. The other 440 have longer ids, EVSE ids beginning GB*BPP* (6 EVSEs AT*HTB*), suboperator "bp pulse", time_zone Europe/London and opening times of 24 hours, give each connector's power, and name no tariff for any connector.Both are shown. Where max_electric_power is 0 the power is worked out from max_voltage and max_amperage, as for any connector with no power figure. Connectors that name no tariff show "Price unknown"; no price is guessed. The time zone is not used on the map, which shows every time in UK time.Openevidence
2026-10-30Data quirkOf the 4,794 EVSEs on 2026-10-30, 1,089 gave the status INOPERATIVE and 634 OUTOFORDER. At 267 of the 1,136 locations every EVSE gave one of the two (199 of the 696 locations with numeric ids, 68 of the other 440).The statuses are shown as published, dated, and both are shown as reported out of service. Whether some of these chargers have been removed is not known.Openevidence
2026-10-30Data quirkTwo connectors give figures that do not fit their type: one Type 1 (IEC_62196_T1) connector gives power_type DC at 400 V and 16 A, and one Type 2 single-phase connector gives 0 A.Both are shown as published. The DC Type 1 connector's power is worked out as 6.4 kW; the 0 A connector's power is unknown.Openevidence
2026-10-30Data quirk33 locations (70 EVSEs, in and around London) have names beginning "[E-TAXI ONLY]", such as "[E-TAXI ONLY] Mill Hill East Station Car Park". No parking restriction or other field in the feed says they are for taxis only.By the repository owner's decision of 2026-10-30 (ADR 0023, amended), "taxi only" is one of the name phrases in pipeline/access.py, so these locations are hidden on the map unless a visitor asks to see private and restricted chargers, and listed on the feed health page. The data is published unchanged.Openevidence
2026-10-30Data quirkTwo locations are named "IDIADA (PRIVATE)" and "IDIADA bp pulse (PRIVATE)".They are hidden on the map unless a visitor asks to see private and restricted chargers, by the name phrases in pipeline/access.py (ADR 0023), and listed on the feed health page.Openevidence
2026-10-07DocumentationEco-Movement's PCPR API documentation gives two different defaults for the page size (1000 on the getting started page, 1 in the endpoint reference), and its user guide says date_from works for locations only while the tariffs reference lists it.This project will always send an explicit page size and test date_from before relying on it.Openevidence
2026-10-07DocumentationThe rate limits are published, but the documentation does not say whether they apply per key or per address, what kind of time window is used, or what response is sent when a limit is reached.Until 2026-10-29 this project assumed the strictest reading: one allowance shared by every endpoint and every operator on this host, so 125 seconds between any two requests to it. Since then, by the repository owner's decision recorded in limits_per_key, each token keeps its own 125 second gap, shared by its endpoints, with requests to the host still sent one at a time.Openevidence

DateTypeWhat was foundHow this project handles itStatusEvidence
2026-10-23Differs from OCPI 2.2.1Locations and tariffs carry country_code GB but no party_id, which OCPI 2.2.1 requires.Record ids use "unknown" for the party, from this file, until the operator's own data gives one.Openevidence
2026-10-23Differs from OCPI 2.2.1Every EVSE status is "idle" (52 EVSEs on 2026-10-23), which is not one of the OCPI 2.2.1 EVSE status values.Shown as status unknown, as for any value outside OCPI 2.2.1, until 2026-10-29. From 2026-10-30, by the repository owner's decision recorded in status_from_connectors, the status is taken from the connectors' own status where they all give the same OCPI 2.2.1 status, and is unknown otherwise.Openevidence
2026-10-23Differs from OCPI 2.2.1Tariff prices are sent as text, for example "0.59", where OCPI 2.2.1 uses a number. Each states its VAT rate (20 for the GBP tariffs).Read as the number written.Openevidence
2026-10-23Data quirkEvery tariff also carries a "price_display" field, which is not in OCPI 2.2.1, equal to its price as written: for example price "0.59" with VAT 20 and price_display "£0.59" (12 of 12 tariffs on 2026-10-23). OCPI 2.2.1 gives prices excluding VAT, so read that way the price is 70.8p per kWh including VAT, while the display price suggests 59p. Which one drivers pay is not stated.Each price is shown as written, read as including VAT as the display price shows it, by the repository owner's decision of 2026-10-23 (vat_in_price above), and the price says it was read that way. The tariffs are published unchanged.Openevidence
2026-10-23Differs from OCPI 2.2.1Connectors give power_type "fast" (48 connectors) or "ultrarapid" (3) and no standard (51), and locations give the two-letter country codes "GB" and "SK" in country. OCPI 2.2.1 uses AC_1_PHASE, AC_3_PHASE or DC for power_type, a ConnectorType for standard and a three-letter ISO 3166-1 code for country.Each is recorded as unknown, as for any value outside OCPI 2.2.1. Whether a location is in the UK is decided from its coordinates, not from country. From 2026-10-30, by the repository owner's decision recorded in socket_types, a connector's plug type is read from its socket_type (next finding).Openevidence
2026-10-23Data quirk15 locations on 2026-10-23: 13 in the UK and 2 in Slovakia, with one EUR tariff. 5 of the UK locations are marked publish false. One location in Slovakia, "Nabito Lab", has no coordinates. Of the rest, three ("Archived Station", "Canalside Farm Stafford" and "Aston Marina", in Stafford and Stone) share the same rounded coordinates, 52.81 and -2.01.Locations marked publish false are not kept, and the one with no coordinates is skipped. The other Slovak location is left off the map, and the EUR tariff shows "Price unknown". The three locations sharing coordinates are kept as published.Openevidence
2026-10-23Data quirk"Kiln Farm Station" has a Milton Keynes postcode (MK11 3LW) and coordinates 52.04 and .82. Milton Keynes is at about longitude -0.82, west of the Greenwich meridian, so the minus sign appears to be missing; as published the point is in Suffolk.Shown at longitude -0.82, by the repository owner's decision of 2026-10-23 (swapped_coordinates above), with the operator's own figures kept and the correction shown on the map.Openevidence
2026-10-30Differs from OCPI 2.2.1Each connector gives two fields that are not in OCPI 2.2.1: socket_type ("Type 2" on 50 and "CCS 2" on 2 of the 52 connectors at published locations) and status ("Available" 44, "Charging" 3, "Unknown" 4, "Unavailable" 1). Every EVSE's own status is "idle".By the repository owner's decisions of 2026-10-30 recorded in socket_types and status_from_connectors, "Type 2" is read as IEC_62196_T2 and "CCS 2" as IEC_62196_T2_COMBO where a connector gives no standard, and a charge point's status is taken from its connectors where they all give the same OCPI 2.2.1 status. Two charge points have connectors that differ (IBIS Derby, eight sockets, one charging and one "Unavailable"; EZ charge HQ, three, one charging), so their status stays unknown. "Unavailable" is not an OCPI 2.2.1 status, so it is never read as one.Openevidence
2026-10-30Data quirkAt "EZ charge HQ" one charge point has two "CCS 2" connectors and one "Type 2" connector, each with max_electric_power 150000 (150 kW) and power_type "ultrarapid".Shown as published, a Type 2 connector at 150 kW; neither value is changed. Whether that connector charges at 150 kW needs testing.Openevidence
char.gy
DateTypeWhat was foundHow this project handles itStatusEvidence
2026-10-07Differs from OCPI 2.2.1EVSE statuses are published as WORKING, and in a live run on 2026-10-07 also as FAULTED. Neither is a value in the OCPI 2.2.1 Status list.Shown as status unknown rather than guessed until 2026-10-09. Since then, by the repository owner's decision recorded in nonstandard_statuses, WORKING is shown as "Working (free or in use, not stated)", never as available, because regulation 10(6)(a) uses "working" to mean an OCPI status of available, charging or reserved. FAULTED is shown as reported out of service.Openevidence
2026-10-07Data quirkTariffs are published in three currencies (EUR, USD and GBP). Locations refer only to the GBP tariff.Only GBP prices are shown, in pence per kWh, which is how regulation 10(6)(d)(iv) describes the price in reference data. Other currencies show as price unknown and are never converted.Openevidence
2026-10-07Data quirkTariff price components give no VAT figure. OCPI 2.2.1 says an omitted VAT means no VAT is applicable.Until 2026-10-22, VAT was not guessed: prices were shown as published, which OCPI defines as excluding VAT, and marked "excluding VAT, VAT not stated". Since then VAT at 20% is added, by the repository owner's decision recorded in vat_not_stated (see the finding of 2026-10-22).Openevidence
2026-10-07AccessThe feed sends no Access-Control-Allow-Origin header, so web browsers cannot read it directly.The map will use data fetched by the pipeline, not requests from visitors' browsers.Openevidence
2026-10-08AccessIn the first daily run from a GitHub Actions runner, the locations feed answered HTTP 403 to the request for offset 2900, after 58 pages had been fetched about 2.8 seconds apart. The same request answered normally from another network the same evening. The server returns at most 50 locations a page, so a full fetch takes about 120 requests for locations alone.char.gy was left out of that run. The gap between requests was raised from 2 to 6 seconds the same day, and the next run (37856014933, the same evening) fetched every page and published 5,948 locations.Resolved 2026-10-08evidence
2026-10-09AccessA one-off full fetch made on 2026-10-09 to check coordinates, from a network other than GitHub's and with the 4 second gap, was answered HTTP 403 for offset 2700, after 54 pages. The daily run of the same day fetched every page.The check stopped there and was not retried. All of char.gy's mapped locations were checked from the published map instead. The one location left off the map in that day's run (listed in that run's manifest) could not be looked at and needs checking in a later run. The daily run's gap is unchanged, since the owner's rule is about refusals in the daily run.Openevidence
2026-10-09AccessIn a daily run started by hand on 2026-10-09 at 17:51 UTC from a GitHub Actions runner, with the 4 second gap, the locations feed answered HTTP 403 for offset 2800, after 56 pages. Across the three refusals so far (offsets 2900, 2700 and 2800, with gaps of about 2.8 and 4 seconds, from two networks) the feed refused after 54 to 58 requests each time, while the run with a 6 second gap fetched all of about 120 pages. This suggests a limit on the number of requests in a period of some minutes rather than a block on GitHub's network. That is inferred from these runs; char.gy states no limit.char.gy was left out of that run's map. The gap went back to 6 seconds, as recorded under rate_limit. Since ADR 0018, a refusal part-way through a run is followed by a wait of 5 minutes and a wider gap before the page is asked for again, at most twice, and if the fetch still fails the map keeps showing the last good copy with its date.Openevidence
2026-10-22Data quirkOf the 12 operators fetched in the daily run of 22 October 2026, 10 give a VAT rate on their prices and 2, char.gy and Clenergy EV, give none. char.gy's own pricing page gives 39p, 59p and 54p per kWh where the feed gives 32.5p, 49.17p and 45p, so each feed price is the page price less 20%. VAT in prices

OCPI 2.2.1 prices exclude VAT, and each price can give the VAT rate that applies to it. Where every price in a tariff gives a rate, this project adds it and shows the price including VAT. A price with no rate is read by OCPI as having no VAT, but VAT on public charging in the UK is at the standard rate of 20%, so such a price is likely to be lower than what a driver pays.

In the daily run of 22 October 2026, 10 of the 12 operators fetched gave a VAT rate on their prices, and char.gy and Clenergy EV gave none. char.gy's own pricing page gives each of its prices 20% higher than its feed does. Clenergy EV's website gives no charging prices, but 120 of the 126 prices per kWh in its feed come to a whole penny once 20% is added. The details are in each operator's findings above.

The repository owner decided on 22 October 2026 that prices on the map should all include VAT, so they can be compared. For each operator listed here, VAT at the rate shown is added to every price that gives no rate, and the price says this project added it. A price with no rate from any other operator is shown as published and marked "VAT not stated". The tariffs themselves are published unchanged.

  • Aldi Stores Limited: 20%, decided 2026-10-29 (evidence)
  • Allego: 20%, decided 2026-10-29 (evidence)
  • Blink Charging: 20%, decided 2026-10-29 (evidence)
  • bp pulse: 20%, decided 2026-10-30 (evidence)
  • char.gy: 20%, decided 2026-10-22 (evidence)
  • Clenergy EV: 20%, decided 2026-10-22 (evidence)
  • Drax Group: 20%, decided 2026-10-29 (evidence)
  • drax-Ista: 20%, decided 2026-10-29 (evidence)
  • evpoint: 20%, decided 2026-10-29 (evidence)
  • GFX-GB Drax: 20%, decided 2026-10-29 (evidence)
  • Globe EV: 20%, decided 2026-10-23 (evidence)
  • Lidl: 20%, decided 2026-10-29 (evidence)
  • RAW Charging: 20%, decided 2026-10-29 (evidence)
  • Roam: 20%, decided 2026-10-26 (evidence)
  • Shell Recharge: 20%, decided 2026-10-29 (evidence)
  • Southampton Apartment Hotels: 20%, decided 2026-10-29 (evidence)
  • Travis Perkins: 20%, decided 2026-10-29 (evidence)
  • Volvo: 20%, decided 2026-10-29 (evidence)
  • WCCC Edgbaston: 20%, decided 2026-10-29 (evidence)

A feed can state a VAT rate on prices that seem to include VAT already: BrightGreen's display price equals the price with no VAT added, and some feeds give every price in whole pence as published, which adding the rate would not. Adding the rate would then show a price higher than the operator seems to charge. On 23 and 29 October 2026 the repository owner decided that for each operator listed here, the VAT its feed states is read as already in its prices: each price is shown as published, including VAT, and says so. This is inferred from the figures, not stated by the operators.

  • BrightGreen: decided 2026-10-23 (evidence)
  • ESB ecars (Northern Ireland): decided 2026-10-29 (evidence)
  • ESB Energy: decided 2026-10-29 (evidence)
  • Pod: decided 2026-10-29 (evidence)

Private and restricted chargers

The repository owner decided on 11 October 2026 that chargers marked as not for public use are hidden on the map unless a visitor ticks "Show chargers marked private or restricted" in the filters. OCPI 2.2.1 has no field that says a location is private, and no feed read so far marks one in any other field, so a location is marked only when the operator's own name for it contains one of these phrases, as whole words in any mix of capitals:

  • "private use", "private access" or "private only", as in "Private Use Only"
  • "private" at the end of the name or in brackets, as in "Site - PRIVATE"
  • "not public", "not for public use" or "non-public"
  • "no public access"
  • "restricted access" or "restricted use"
  • "staff only", "fleet only", "employees only", "residents only", "tenants only", "members only" or "permit holders only", with or without "use" before "only"
  • "staff car park"
  • "taxi only" or "taxis only", as in "[E-TAXI ONLY]"

Words that often name a place rather than a rule, such as "Fleet" in Fleetwood Road, "Depot", "Residential", "Visitor Centre" or "Blue Badge holders only", are not counted. A marked location is published unchanged: its details say which words were found, and the feed health page lists every location marked in the latest run. See the feed health page.

Separately, where the owner has decided so for an operator (see Sources above), a location where the operator marks every charge point for taxis only is hidden in the same way, and each such charge point is labelled "Taxis only".

Latest runs

The results of each daily run, with counts, problems logged and a 30-day history, are on the feed health page.

OEVM is a personal, experimental project for learning about AI tools. Much of its code and text was written with an AI assistant, and it will contain mistakes. Charger data comes from the operators and other sources credited on every record, and remains their work. Everything is provided "as is", with no warranty or guarantee of any kind. Always check prices and availability at the charger.
